# LLP 3000.036.001: Merged pages for owner-or-X lists

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Review (revision 7 implemented in `sb4-loop`; PAGE2 §15 and PAGE3 §16 implemented, each with both final reviews READY)
**Systems:** Snapback 4 language (`snapback4-lang`: intent planning `bind/intent_branches.rs`, planned-merge binding `bind/program/views.rs`, `N_NEVER_COMPLETE`, the reads/indexes/sharing/recipes/cheatsheet cards), core (`snapback4-core`: `ir.rs` lane identities, `engine/branch_merge.rs`, the page walk's grant arm and frontier, `rules::branch_merge_lazy` and `covering_grant`, cursor versions 4 and 5), the device (`snapback4-device`, both stores), the Lean model (`snapback4/lean`: OPEN C8 and C2, `Proofs/Branches.lean`), the difftest generator (`widen.rs`) and its Lean features
**Author:** Claude Opus 5.5 for Charlie Cheever (workstream MERGE)
**Date:** 2026-10-12
**Revised:** 2026-10-15 (revision 7: §18 preserves a standalone static grant disjunct as its guarded lane beside membership branches while retaining static points inside conjunctions); 2026-10-14 (§18 PAGE5 amendment); 2026-10-14 (§17 PAGE4 amendment); 2026-10-14 (PAGE3 §16: party/static-role, pure-membership and positive compound pages; original-prefix bounds, checked hidden-parent rewrites, full-union device pages and separate model admission gates; Muse and fresh Codex both READY at `65d505289f`); 2026-10-13 (PAGE2: exact unique membership witnesses no longer need a separately declared discovery cap; unbounded inverted discovery remains all-or-nothing under the meter; row-own-`id` and parent-owner lanes; exact cap advice for shapes a cap can repair; §15); 2026-10-13 (the Lean classifier and executable model share prefix-normalized Boolean guards without equating nullable `null` and `false`; known-false compound guards and negative fixed literals use one canonical form; §9, §14); 2026-10-12 (PAGE: resolves Q6 with capped owner-or-membership lanes; one cursor, discovery-cut and device semantics; §14); 2026-10-12 (CUT: inclusive interrupted-predicate retry and held-group prefix-meter parity; LLP 3000.007 §20.24.1, §20.24.7); 2026-10-12 (UNION integration: synced unions retain ordinary per-arm scans; the online grant lane defers membership probes on devices; §7); 2026-10-12 (revision 6: round-5 GPT size-bound and nested-note findings; bounded descriptor digest, portable-key fallback, case-1-only existence wording; §13); 2026-10-12 (revision 5: GPT's round-4 review, NOT READY; a member through a reference is not total, the capability walks the whole body, the token's scope is a digest of the lanes' pins; §13); 2026-10-12 (revision 4: round-3 reviews, both NOT READY; case 2 always plans its grant lane, which the system reads whole; `exists` merges case 1 only; branch tokens are scoped to every lane's pins; capability `branch-merge-v1` fences older devices; the Lean model normalizes the planner's pins; §13); 2026-10-12 (revision 3: round-2 reviews, GPT NOT READY and Muse READY WITH CHANGES; the grant lane is planned as the read as written, clock arithmetic is not total, the frontier capture is armed and scoped to its lane, the eager path's foreign token restarts the read, and the Lean model mirrors the planner's guard, helper, existence and lane-count conditions; §13); 2026-10-12 (revision 2: merged with 0.4.23, whose LN workstream made a disjunct the page's pins decide a page guard (LLP 3000.036 §16.3); case 2 now takes such guards beside parties, §2, §3, §9, §13); 2026-10-12 (revision 1: implemented; folds the blind GPT and Muse reviews of the draft, both NOT READY — totality, inclusive shape positions, the frontier continuation, order-column and index-width exclusions, seats on devices, the encoding and the downgrade; dispositions in §13); 2026-10-12 (draft for review, first numbered 3000.038, renumbered here because sibling workstreams took 3000.038)
**Related:** [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (§7 2026-09-13 "wide uniform merges read heads"), [LLP 3000.007.000](./3000.007.000-merge-position-and-drain-cut-continuation.spec.md) (the merge position and the drain-cut continuation), [LLP 3000.027](./3000.027-top-n-answer-is-complete.rfc.md), [LLP 3000.035](./3000.035-quarry-semantics-in-lean.plan.md) (the Lean model), [LLP 3000.036](./3000.036-what-the-app-farm-asks-of-the-rule-language.rfc.md) §12.1 (readable grants), §12.2, §13.2, §16.1 (owner-or-X lists and the complete form), [LLP 3000.036.000](./3000.036.000-sharing-shapes-at-sync-and-union-audiences.rfc.md) §4 (union audiences, orthogonal), the reviews `llp/reviews/3000.036.001-merged-pages.{gpt,muse}.md`, the ticket `issues/20261012-snapback4-device-group-page-rule-probe-meter.md`

## 0. Summary

A list such as

```quarry
query myTasks(c: cursor ?):
  return tasks where .owner = viewer or .assignee = viewer last 50 by .at after c
```

over `read <- .owner = viewer or .assignee = viewer` was never `complete`.
The engine walked one index per page; neither `[owner, at, id]` nor
`[assignee, at, id]` holds every row the caller may read, so the page was
planned on `[at, id]`, checked the rule per row, and could not tell "no more"
from "more you may not read". `check` printed `N_NEVER_COMPLETE` with a
verified per-party rewrite (LLP 3000.036 §12.2, §13.2, §16.1). Across six farm
waves 12, 19 and 16 lanes reported the shape.

Now the one-query form is complete. When a query page's predicate, or its
table's read rule, is a disjunction of branches each of which pins an index
prefix that decides the read rule, the planner reads it as a k-way merge of
the branch ranges in the page's order. A row matching several branches is
emitted once. Each branch's rows are decided by its own pins, so the merged
page is `complete` exactly when every branch's walk finishes. The cursor is
one position in the shared order, which every branch resumes strictly after
(inclusively, for an unfinished first returned shape); its token is a new
version naming the branch set. A meter cut continues from the frontier every
branch has examined. Devices run the same plan with the existing per-scan
coverage rules. The Lean model's C8 gains the rule, with a coverage proof.

Owner-or-staff (`.owner = viewer or exists staff[viewer]`) is the same plan
with one branch guarded by the readable grant of §12.1: held, that branch is
the whole page; not held, it contributes nothing and the owner branch is the
page. A staff page is complete for staff (as before) and an owner's page is
complete for everyone else (new). The guide's owner-or-staff and two-party
recipes are now single queries.

## 1. What the builders met

LLP 3000.036 records the shape in waves 2–6: `#12` (12 lanes), `#3b`
(19 lanes) and the §14.2 and §16.1 notes (16 lanes). The lanes either pasted
the per-party rewrite (two queries, two cursors, a client-side merge and
de-duplication, two loading states) or kept the one-query form and shipped a
list that always said "there may be more". The engine already merges ordered
lanes (LLP 3000 §7, 2026-09-13) and already has the position that makes a
merge resumable (LLP 3000.007.000 §1); this RFC lets one merge read several
indexes of one table.

## 2. The rule

Definitions, over the checked schema:

- An **equality pin** in a conjunction is a conjunct `.col = e` (either
  side) or a bare boolean column (`.flag`, `not .flag`), where `e` reads no
  row column, `.col` is a plain column of the table and not `id`. On a
  nullable column it counts only when `e` is `viewer`: a branch never seeks a
  null the original filter would reject.
- A **party disjunct** of a read rule is `.col = viewer` (either side) on a
  principal column of the row.
- A **guard disjunct** reads no column of the row (§12.1: a role probe, a
  seat such as `viewer = 'service:audit'`), or reads only columns the page's
  own pins fix (§16.3: `exists boards[.board, owner = viewer]` under `.board =
  b`). It is **usable** when `rules::page_guard_decision` takes it: no `not`,
  and every row it probes readable by whoever its key names.
- A prefix **decides** the read rule when every column the rule reads is
  pinned (with the self-witness proof of LLP 3000.007 §16), or the rule is
  true by the pins' form: `allow`, a `.col = viewer` disjunct pinned to
  `viewer`, a `.col = 'x'` disjunct pinned to `'x'`, and `and`/`or` of
  those. This is the core's `rules::rule_true`/`scan_visibility`, the
  binder's `intent_branches::decided` and the model's `decidedPins`.
- A predicate is **total** when it is comparisons (`=`, `!=`, `<`, `<=`,
  `>`, `>=`, `in`) and boolean operators over the row's own columns,
  arguments, literals and `viewer`: its evaluation never refuses and never
  reads. A member of anything but the row (`.meta.ok`, `m.ok`) is not: it
  binds to a read of the referenced row. CUT (2026-10-12) fixes interrupted
  single-index predicates by retrying their row inclusively (LLP 3000.007
  §20.24.1), but does not make such a predicate total: missing/hidden targets
  can still refuse or be unavailable, and distribution changes which reads
  short-circuit. Keep this exclusion and the merge's decided lane frontier.
  Arithmetic is not either,
  not even a time window (`now - 7d` overflows when the clock is at the
  integer bound), so such a page keeps one index. The Lean model has no `in`;
  the emitter names it unsupported.

A **query page** (`t where … first|last N by … [after c]` in a query program;
never in a mutation, job, shared helper or shape, which keep ordinary scans)
is **branch-decided** when all of these hold:

- it names no explicit index or key, does not search (`matching`), has no
  computed order, and reads an ordinary table (not a maintained view, row
  view, ephemeral or timeline table, a `public` declaration or a `read
  through` child), whose read rule is one `read` declaration;
- its own pins do **not** decide the rule (a decided page keeps its plan,
  index and cursor);
- its predicate is total;
- and either
  1. **branches in `where`:** exactly one top-level conjunct is an `or` of
     2–8 alternatives, each holding an equality pin, and each alternative
     with the other conjuncts decides the rule; or
  2. **branches in the read rule:** the rule is an `or` of at least two
     disjuncts, each a party or a usable guard, with at least one party.
     The branches are `base_j and .party_k = viewer` for every base branch
     (case 1's alternatives when `where` has such an `or`, else the whole
     `where`) and party, plus one **grant branch**, the whole `where`, read
     for a caller the rule's guards admit and for the system, which every
     rule admits (a rule of parties alone has no guard: the lane is the
     system's only);
- and every branch plans to an index of at most 8 components whose position
  after its pins is the page's order then `id` (a branch never pins an order
  column: a merge lane keeps such an equality as a seek), with at most 8
  branches.

Otherwise the page keeps its single-index plan and, when undecided,
`N_NEVER_COMPLETE` with its per-party rewrite.

A single party (`read <- .owner = viewer`) is not expanded: its complete form
is the pin the author writes, and expanding it would replan every unpinned
owner page (§12 Q1). 8 is both the index width limit and the point below
which the lazy merge reads a page per lane (`word_merge.rs`); every branch
may be a synthesized index.

### 2.1 Why the merged rows are exactly the readable matches

Write `W` for the predicate and `R` for the read rule; the page returns, in
order, the rows `r` with `W(r) ∧ R(r)`.

- Case 1: `W = rest ∧ (a_1 ∨ … ∨ a_k)`, so `W = ∨_j (a_j ∧ rest)`. Branch
  `j` reads the range its pins select and filters by the rest of `a_j ∧
  rest`; its prefix decides `R`, so every row of the branch is readable
  (verdict true) or none is (verdict false: the branch is empty). The union
  is `∨_j (a_j ∧ rest ∧ R) = W ∧ R`.
- Case 2: `R = ∨_k (.p_k = viewer) ∨ G` with `G` row-independent or reading
  only the page's pinned columns, so for every row the page selects and this
  caller `R(r) = ∨_k (r.p_k = viewer) ∨ g` with `g` one boolean (the guard
  with the pinned values), and
  `W ∧ R = ∨_{j,k} (b_j ∧ .p_k = viewer) ∨ (g ∧ W)`. Each party branch is
  decided true by its pin; the grant branch is all of `W` when `g` holds and
  nothing otherwise, and then it is a superset of every other branch, so the
  plan reads it alone. The Lean model proves the party half
  (`parties_cover_view`): every visible row carries the viewer in a party
  column.

The argument distributes `W` over its alternatives, so it needs `W` total:
with `or (.assignee = viewer and 1 / n = 1)`, a row in both parties skips the
division in the original and would evaluate it in its own branch (GPT
review, finding 1). A branch evaluates the predicate only on rows the
original also reads, so a total predicate gives the same rows.

A row in several branches has the same position in each (§5), so the merge
emits it once. No branch reads a row the caller may not read, so no such row
reaches the merge, the filter, the lookahead or the cursor.

## 3. The plan

**Planning** (`bind/intent_branches.rs`, called from `intent::plan` where the
other lane expansions run). Each branch is an ordinary planned read
(`Requests::plan_lane`): its pins form the prefix (a party pin first, so the
deciding pin is never left residual), the page's order then `id` the suffix;
the planner reuses a declared index with exactly those columns or synthesizes
one. The expansion is dropped, and the read planned as written, when a branch
does not decide the rule (by the binder's check), the branches' order columns
differ, an index would exceed 8 components, or a branch exceeds the portable 2,048-byte key or 512-byte label bound,
or planning a branch raised a diagnostic: a program that compiled before still compiles. The result is the
existing `MergeExpr` with planned lanes, each carrying the branch identity
`$or` (or `$or:grant`) in the field views use for theirs; the planned-merge
binding passes it through to the IR `MergeLane.branch`. No IR type gains a
field, so every existing program's IR and pinned digest is unchanged;
`Merge::branches()` recognises the plan.

**Binding check** (`program/views.rs`). With the core schema in hand, the
binder re-checks the plan with the runtime's own functions: every grant
lane's `rules::covering_grant` (every disjunct that is not a party is a
usable guard of `page_guard_decision` for the lane's pins, §12.1, §16.3) and `rules::branch_merge_lazy` (same table and order, scalar
pins, every non-grant lane `scan_visibility(..).uniform`, a total position,
identical position columns). `covering_grant` compares each non-party
disjunct after the same prefix simplification that produced the page guard;
`allow`, `deny`, `not` and required boolean pins therefore have one identity
through the coverage proof, while a nullable boolean's `null` is never
normalized to `false`. If it fails and the plan has a grant lane, the
read binds as its grant lane alone, which is the read as written: the grant
lane is planned exactly as the single-index page is (not as a merge lane), so
the fallback is 0.4.23's plan, page guard included; when that plan's order
differs from the party lanes' (an equality on the first order column becomes
a pin there), the page is not merged at all (a hidden
roster, `read <- deny` on `staff`, keeps today's plan and note); without a
grant lane the merge stays, sound and, for a lane that is not decided,
incomplete.

**Execution** (`engine/branch_merge.rs`). A branch merge that passes
`branch_merge_lazy` is a lazy merge whose lanes may read different indexes of
one table. Each lane's position is its index components after its prefix,
plus `id` when they do not end in it (LLP 3000.007.000 §1); the binding check
makes the positions comparable, and because they end in the row id, equal
positions are the same row. Grant lanes open first. A grant lane's guard is
decided once with the lane's pinned values (`grant_verdict`, charged as §12.1) and, when it holds, the lane
reads as a decided page (`scan_consumed`'s grant arm, through the runner's
`merge_grant`) and the other lanes are not opened; when it does not hold, the
lane is not read and does not mark the page non-uniform; for the system it
reads every row. Each lane opens with one read of `take + 1` rows; the heap
pops in position order, de-duplicates by id and refills a lane strictly after
its last popped position. A branch merge that is not lazy-eligible takes the
eager path with the same branch descriptor; it is sound and, with a lane
that is not decided, incomplete with no `next`.

**Page guards (LLP 3000.036 §16.3).** Without the merge, 0.4.23 decides a
single-index page once for a caller a pinned guard admits, and walks it per
row (incomplete) for everyone else. With it, a page whose rule is parties and
such guards merges the guard branch with the party branches, so the guard's
holder reads the guard branch alone and everyone else their party branches,
all complete. A page the merge does not cover keeps the single-index guard.

**Older engines.** Older devices refuse a merged backend by its capability
(§5). Were one to run the plan anyway, an engine that does not know the lane
identities (0.4.22) ignores them, so a backend compiled by this release still runs on an older
device: lanes over different indexes take the eager merge, which translates
one cursor across lanes with the same suffix (LLP 3000.007 §20.3) and
de-duplicates by id. It returns the same rows, mints its own cursor form, and
evaluates a grant lane as an ordinary page. For a plan without guards the
pages continue as here. A plan with a guard is only ever run by an older
*device* (servers compile their own IR), and a guard's table never syncs
except for a seat, which the device trusts: a role or parent-probe guard's
table is online only, so the older device's answer is unknown and the client
asks the server, never paging the device's incomplete answer (GPT round 2,
finding 2). `an_older_engine_reads_the_plan_as_an_eager_merge`
runs the plan with the identities stripped and compares every page.

## 4. Completeness and disclosure

- **Exact.** The page is `complete` when no lane is non-uniform, the meter
  did not cut, and the existing query-level conditions hold (position loss,
  device coverage, horizons). By §2.1 its rows are then exactly the first N
  readable matches after the cursor.
- **Nothing outside the rule.** Every lane is admitted by its own prefix
  verdict (`prefix_verdict`) or its grant before any row is read; a false
  verdict reads nothing. This is the admission a direct page on that prefix
  takes.
- **What `complete: true` and the meter reveal.** For a party or `where`
  branch, what a direct page on that prefix reveals: a verdict decided by
  facts the caller could read. For a grant branch, whether the grant holds,
  which §12.1 already reveals through a staff page's `complete` and which
  `grant_decision` restricts to grants whose every fact the caller can read.
retains native fallback semantics instead of claiming a complete owner pin
on an authored fixed index. Both review counterexamples have regressions;
the generator includes argument-valued pinned pairs.

Delivered-history integration: an acquired device history is an audience in
its own right under LLP 3000.007 §18.2, even after live membership expires.
A membership merge on such a target selects its existing whole-range system
descriptor on the device without changing the actual viewer. It retains the
authored W, acquisition, lifetime, retention, meters and shared cursor scope;
only current-source rediscovery is bypassed. Server execution is unchanged.
`branch-pages-v3` fences this device interpretation. Existing expiry/paged
snapshot history regressions run on both stores. NARR retains ownership of
P9's general unbounded-sync/empty-partition narration; this compatibility
correction arose from PAGE4 named-order planning.

## 18. PAGE5: bounded products, fixed guards and exact vacancies

Commissioned 2026-10-14 for wave-12 fix #12. PAGE5 extends the bounded PAGE
planner only where every admitted lane still has a constructive exhaustion
argument. It does not turn arbitrary Boolean authorization into a complete
query plan.

**Bounded products.** The planner distributes independent positive
alternatives in the authorization rule across conjunctions before it
classifies the resulting lanes.
For example, two exact principal alternatives and three exact status
alternatives produce six lanes, each retaining both selected predicates and
the original query predicate. Distribution is all-or-nothing and remains
inside the existing eight-lane limit, including the system lane. Overflow,
negative alternatives and a residual second varying witness retain the
analyzer-limitation fallback.

The same bounded Cartesian construction applies to positive membership PAGE
query filters. Every resulting branch must contain an eligible equality pin,
the system descriptor plus every party or membership descriptor must remain
within eight, and every original predicate stays in its lane. A product that
pins the membership witness's varying outer field, overflows, or leaves a
branch without a usable pin stays on the ordinary incomplete scan. The Lean
classifier constructs every factor before applying those same exclusions; it
never treats a failed product as an undistributed complete lane or a second
`or` predicate as absent. A non-alternative equality or required bare Boolean
shared by every product branch supplies an eligible pin to each branch.
Repeated party leaves name one physical audience prefix and count once per
product branch.

**Fixed grants beside one varying grant.** One dynamic membership witness may
be accompanied by one or more fixed `exists` guards, independent of authored
conjunction grouping. A fixed guard is admissible only when
the lane pins every field term in its target key and the target exposes a
required, total, unique point that is caller-readable after those exact lane
pins are substituted. The target must have no hidden source, read-through boundary,
`not before` boundary or expiry. The compiler substitutes the exact lane pin
into the guard and keeps that complete guard in the generated query filter;
an already-static point such as `exists roles[viewer]` needs no substitution
but remains in that filter. The residual may contain three or more fixed
guards; its exact short-circuit conjunction chain remains necessary in full.
The core validator independently matches it to the original rule expression
and lane pin. Thus the generated guard observes every fixed grant that could
authorize the authored rule. A hidden, timed, non-total or extra-equality
guard remains incomplete. Two varying grant sources also remain outside this
fragment.

A standalone static grant disjunct remains its ordinary guarded lane beside
party and membership lanes. It is not a residual sibling of the membership
witness: if it admits the caller, that guarded lane reads the whole authored
range; otherwise it contributes no rows. Only a static point in the same
conjunction as another atom enters the retained-filter proof above. This
distinction preserves complete `static grant or membership` and
`party or static grant or membership` pages without dropping any conjunct.

**Exact vacancy lanes.** The server may invert a top-level
`not exists holds[.id]` alternative when `holds.id` is a required, unique,
plain point and the paged table is online-only. Its vacancy lane scans the
same full ordered outer range as the system lane and privately authorizes each
candidate against the original rule; it publishes completion only after that
authorized walk is exhausted. A predicate-meter or authorization-walk cut
therefore produces an incomplete page. Filtered vacancy reads, synchronized
outer tables, compound or non-`id` keys, non-unique points and any shortcut
around the ordinary per-row authorization check keep the fallback. The target
may itself be hidden or timed: the private server walk evaluates the authored
rule's system probe for each candidate, then exposes only the authorized outer
row. This is a vacancy predicate over the outer row, not a claim that a private
target's absence is globally observable.
The intrinsic `id` point is always required and unique even though schema
column and unique lists omit it; other vacancy keys require both declarations.

**Index rewrite note.** When every missing rule field is already fixed by an
independent exact query equality, the note prints one concrete declaration
using the actual table and collision-free index name, followed by the complete
rewritten query using that same index. The rewrite locates the focal read by
its source-map span, even when an earlier read uses the same index name, and
the binder recompiles the printed declaration and query together in tests.
It omits the repair when the added components or emitted label would exceed
the portable index bounds, or when authored bracket keys would change column
meaning under the prepended prefix.
When those exact equalities do not establish the rule prefix, the note
does not offer this rewrite.

**Compatibility and model.** `branch-pages-v4` fences the new validator
interpretations: fixed residual point guards and exact vacancy lanes. A device
that only understands `branch-pages-v3` must refuse this IR before decoding or
executing it; bounded products reuse the already validated positive-branch
lanes, and existing PAGE4 plans keep their prior capability. The Lean surface
classifier mirrors the three admissions and the private authorization walk.
Generated differential cases exercise the new lane families on native,
simulator and device hosts. Forms outside the fragment remain honest analyzer
limitations rather than completeness claims.

-    // The IR preserves short-circuit `a and b` as a tiny Boolean block.
-    // Accept only that exact lowering: both operands are necessary, while an
-    // `or` block assigns on the false branch and cannot establish either one.
-    if let Expr::Block { body, value } = filter
-        && let [crate::ir::Stmt::Let { name, value: left }, crate::ir::Stmt::If {

+    // The compiler lowers a left-associated `a and b and c` into successive

+        while at < body.len() {
+            let [crate::ir::Stmt::Let { name, value: left }, crate::ir::Stmt::If {
+                condition: Expr::Variable(condition), then_body, else_body }] = &body[at..at + 2] else { return false };
+            let [crate::ir::Stmt::Assign { name: assigned, value: right }] = then_body.as_slice() else { return false };
+            if !else_body.is_empty() || condition != name || assigned != name { return false; }
+            if let Some(previous) = previous {
+                if !matches!(left, Expr::Variable(input) if input == previous) { return false; }
+            } else {
+                matched |= fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, left, rule, pins);
+            }
+            matched |= fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, right, rule, pins);
+            previous = Some(name);
+            at += 2;
+        }
+        if !matches!((value.as_ref(), previous), (Expr::Variable(result), Some(previous)) if result == previous) { return false; }
+        return matched;
     }
     let (Expr::Exists(read), RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. }) = (filter, rule) else { return false };
     let Some(target) = schema.table(table) else { return false };

+/// Four binary equality factors require sixteen membership lanes and must not
+/// collapse into one undistributed complete descriptor after product overflow.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — alternative overflow retains the ordinary incomplete scan.
+pub fn page_membership_filter_overflow(seed: u64) -> Case {
+    let mut case = base(seed);

+    case.program.tables[0].effects = None;
+    case.program.tables[0].residency = Residency::Online;

+    let access = case.program.tables.iter_mut().find(|table| table.name == "access").unwrap();
+    access.rule = Rule::Allow; access.effects = None; access.residency = Residency::Public;
+    access.expire = None; access.not_before = None;
+    case.program.body = Body::Read { kind: Read::Page, take: 1, descending: seed % 2 == 0,
+        filter: false, owner: false, project: None };

+    case.program.tables[0].residency = Residency::Online;

+    case.program.tables[0].columns.push(column("rank", Type::Int));

+    case.program.residual = Some("(.score > 0 or .score > 10) and (.rank > 0 or .rank > 10)".into());

+    for (table, row) in &mut case.rows { if table == "items" {
+        row["region"] = json!(if seed % 3 == 0 { "south" } else { "north" });
+        row["rank"] = json!((seed % 15) as i64);
+    }}
+    case

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/smoke.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/smoke.rs
index 7d6a20da18..f27427ef2c 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/smoke.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/smoke.rs
@@ -796,3 +796,33 @@ fn membership_page_with_two_equality_alternative_factors_is_complete_in_lean_and
         }
     }
 }
+
+#[test]
+fn page5_review_boundaries_agree_in_generated_native_and_lean_cases() {
+    let have_model = model_built();
+    for seed in 0..4 {
+        for (case, complete, model_boundary) in [
+            (snapback4_difftest::generate::page_membership_filter_overflow(seed), false, Some("page-membership-filter-product")),
+            (snapback4_difftest::generate::page_membership_shared_filter_product(seed), true, None),
+            (snapback4_difftest::generate::page5_implicit_id_vacancy(seed), true, None),
+        ] {
+            let source = case.program.source();
+            execute::compile(&source).unwrap_or_else(|error| panic!("seed {seed}: {error}\n{source}"));
+            let mut engine = execute::engine(&case, &source).unwrap();
+            let outcome = execute::execute(&mut engine, &case, "run", &case.args);
+            assert_eq!(outcome.value["complete"], complete, "seed {seed}: {outcome:?}\n{source}");
+            let mut report = Report::default(); campaign::check(&case, &mut report, |_| {});
+            assert!(!report.failed(), "seed {seed}: {report:?}\n{source}");
+            if have_model {
+                let checked = snapback4_difftest::lean::compare(&case, &source).unwrap();
+                if let Some(reason) = model_boundary {
+                    assert!(matches!(&checked.verdict, Verdict::Unsupported(actual) if actual == reason),
+                        "seed {seed}: {:?}\n{source}", checked.verdict);
+                    assert_eq!(checked.executed, 0, "seed {seed}: model boundary executed\n{source}");
+                } else {
+                    assert!(matches!(checked.verdict, Verdict::Pass), "seed {seed}: {:?}\n{source}", checked.verdict);
+                }
+            }
+        }
+    }
+}

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit/lean/pages.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit/lean/pages.rs

 //! Physical page eligibility derived solely from Quarry surface declarations.
 use super::*;
 
+enum PagePins {
+    Plain(Vec<String>),
+    Product { system: Vec<String>, branches: Vec<Vec<String>> },
+    Invalid,
+}
+
+/// Reconstruct the native planner's bounded Cartesian product from the
+/// authored filter.  This stays surface-derived: the executable model cannot
+/// trust the binder's proof metadata when deciding whether comparison is safe.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — every modeled PAGE filter lane has its own exact physical prefix.
+fn page_pins(table: &Table, read: &ReadExpr) -> PagePins {
+    let Some(filter) = read.filter.as_deref() else { return PagePins::Plain(Vec::new()) };
+    let mut conjuncts = Vec::new();
+    flatten_and(filter, &mut conjuncts);
+    let system = model_pin_columns(table, &conjuncts, &read.order_keys);
+    let mut branches = vec![Vec::new()];
+    let mut distributed = false;
+    for conjunct in &conjuncts {
+        let mut alternatives = Vec::new();
+        flatten_or(conjunct, &mut alternatives);
+        distributed |= alternatives.len() > 1;
+        if branches.len().saturating_mul(alternatives.len()) > 8 { return PagePins::Invalid; }
+        branches = branches.into_iter().flat_map(|prefix| alternatives.iter().map(move |alternative| {
+            let mut lane = prefix.clone();
+            flatten_and(alternative, &mut lane);
+            lane
+        })).collect();
+    }
+    if !distributed || branches.len() < 2 { return PagePins::Plain(system); }
+    if branches.iter().any(|branch| !branch.iter().any(|atom| page_pin(table, atom))) {
+        return PagePins::Invalid;
+    }
+    PagePins::Product {
+        system,
+        branches: branches.into_iter().map(|branch| model_pin_columns(table, &branch, &read.order_keys)).collect(),
+    }
+}
+
+fn page_pin(table: &Table, expression: &Expr) -> bool {
+    let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = expression else { return false };
+    if op != "=" { return false; }
+    let (column, value) = if let Some(column) = column_of(left) { (column, right.as_ref()) }
+        else if let Some(column) = column_of(right) { (column, left.as_ref()) }
+        else { return false };
+    column != "id" && model_independent(value) && table.cols.iter().find(|field| field.name == column)
+        .is_some_and(|field| !field.nullable || matches!(value, Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "viewer"))
+}

snapback4/AGENTS.md
# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.
# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A per-query result cache.** No range receipts, validators, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7).
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
- **No standing panel** except the two blind reviews LLP 3000 decision 4 names (partition inversion; the meter's cut on non-uniform scans). The author explicitly commissioned the one-off LLP 3000.008 panel and disjoint Astra implementation lanes on 2026-09-14; its bounded disposition is recorded in that umbrella and LLP 3000 §7.
- **No new LLP number** without first extending LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000; no spec without running code (`rules/RULES.md`: only implemented specs bind).
- **No second session on the core** during slice one; lanes take the periphery.
- **No test matrix as the primary harness.** The simulator is; real-browser and device witnesses cover only what it cannot reach.
- **No files over 1,500 lines**, including ones copied from Snapback 2. Split on
  the way in. The one exception is the package's published documents
  (`packages/snapback4/README.md` and its linked references): the rule exists to
  keep a *source* file reviewable, and the 0.2.25 calibration measured the cost of
  applying it to a document — splitting the README was that run's single largest
  scoring loss, because the builder then had four places to look instead of one
  (LLP 3000.007 §20.19). A published document is bounded by whether a builder can
  find one way in, not by a line count; the census that enforces this rule covers
  source, and says so.

## Deliberately worse

- No compatibility with Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 apps, packages or on-disk formats.
- No API stability before the clock; every publish is `0.0.x`.
- Sparse prose documentation; the diagnostics and the recipes carry the load.
- One hosted target, SQLite in the production CLI, one platform binary at a time. A separately enabled Postgres evaluation adapter is not a hosted/deployable target (Moves below).

## Moves

- **2026-10-13 — stored lower lifetimes (`not before`) are commissioned by PAGE2** (LLP 3000.036.003). This unblocks timed disclosure without an application publication job. Per-application jobs that flip a publication flag leave this admitted path; clock expressions in rules, captured lower lifetimes for read-through children, and boundary recounts for maintained data remain excluded. HS owns handshake transport; PAGE2 declares the new semantic capability.

- **2026-10-06 — exact Following history (FH delegated round-1 ruling).**
  Admit a Following-specific server traversal and bounded volatile frontier/replay
  state under LLP 3000.026 §9. The approved design reads exact history online
  from the first page; the SDK will own continuation. The first milestone is internal event metadata
  and the oracle, with no public completeness claim. Taken off: a blanket ban on
  state for this one expiring read. General result caches, resumable queries,
  device traversal, inbox coverage certificates and history-only blocks remain
  excluded. Existing inbox bytes and runtime ceilings remain unchanged.

- **2026-09-26 — server operation latency (LLP 3000.019 §3.7).** Calibration #22
  measured each read once and could not claim repeated p95/degradation testing.
  Unblocks percentile measurements of the application's own load without a
  custom timing harness. Taken off: a latency harness written by the builder;
  general metrics, tracing infrastructure and an admin console remain excluded.

- **2026-09-23 — Quarry states intent moves onto the doing-list** (Charlie:
  "if you need to try making *significant* architectural reworks in order to
  make the data modeling, queries, and scalability be excellent and elegant,
  you can experiment with that"; LLP 3000.013). Unblocks reads that state
  predicates and order, one `view` declaration for derived data, Following
  without caps, jobs and aggregates without author-chosen bounds, an app
  object and an offline web shell. Taken off: index names and author-chosen
  engine numbers on the paved path; the timeline's promotion, pull routes and
  caps; the "Time in rules" refusal is unchanged. Stands: one interpreter, no
  JavaScript in the data plane, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no relevance-ranked
  search.

- **2026-09-16 — the remaining general-purpose program moves onto the doing-list**
  (Charlie: "complete everything ... we'll remove services when we are done
  with the workroom test rather than wait to add them in ... use the fleet";
  LLP 3000.009 §14.7). Unblocks the §7 integrated-service baseline, C3 production
  Postgres, D's authorized database capabilities and measurement-led C2/E
  work while Workroom continues. Taken off: waiting for Workroom before
  starting services, and keeping production Postgres integration outside the
  current implementation program. The completed Workroom record drives a
  retain/remove pass. Stands: one interpreter and managed writer, no managed
  SQL writes, no JavaScript transactions, no SQL in the first Workroom cell,
  no invented performance/real-provider evidence, no expo-build-1000 load,
  and no new publication or managed-service deployment authority.

- **2026-09-16 — application SQL reads move onto the doing-list** (Charlie: "admit application SQL reads"; LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4, increment D). Unblocks reporting and search over authorized projection inputs and declared database-owned tables, with authorization before selection, ordering and limits. Taken off in the same change: the inspection-only exclusion for application SQL and D's pending-author gate. Implementation and qualification remain pending; SQLite and Postgres halves ship together, channels follow candidates, and A1–A3 plus the fixture pack stay first. Stands: no SQL writes into managed data, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no SQL in the first Snapback brief cell, and no publication authorization.

- **2026-09-15 — a Postgres evaluation backend moves onto the doing-list** (author: "Build the evaluation prototype"). Unblocks concrete evaluation of storage agnosticism, interpreter/database round trips and transactional sync ordering. This bounded prototype replaces full CLI/deployment integration as the next backend task; those product surfaces remain off this increment's doing-list. It shares Quarry, rules, maintains and partition logic, but uses an experimental Postgres store. Native SQL exists only as an explicitly separate comparison workload, not an application escape hatch. SQLite remains the production and local-device store. See [the experiment](./experiments/postgres/README.md).

- **2026-09-15 — the server half of LLP 3000.009 moves onto the doing-list** (author: "begin implementation using astra xhigh to code"; LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increments A1–A3). Unblocks the online-only brief's invitation, webhook and scheduled journeys without a second application server: work records whose authority is bound at admission, ids bound to their operation and arguments, declared action results, a generated handler context with a durable record per external step, declared routes and webhooks, scheduled actions under service seats, and `serve` supervising the runtime. Taken off in the same change: "A jobs framework" narrows — actions, effects and scheduled actions get per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation; priorities, worker pools and dead letters stay out — and the actions card's "supervise the worker as an ordinary Node process" leaves. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no identity provider in the engine, and no `ctx.sql`, projections or SQL reads in this increment.

- **2026-09-15 — the Postgres access boundary moves onto the doing-list** (LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increment C1). Unblocks measuring Postgres behind a batched access boundary instead of row-at-a-time calls: transaction-scoped prefetch, a pipelined commit tail, one portable index-key bound checked on both stores, and the corpus, conformance and device suites on both stores. Taken off in the same change: the evaluation prototype's single-adapter framing, in which the only next step was an unbatched store. Stands: Postgres is not a hosted or deployable target; its refusals of assets, jobs, actions and schedules, and `serve`, `deploy` and backup on Postgres, wait for increment C3.

- **2026-09-13 — word search, effects, service seats and presence move onto the doing-list** (author: "add all the things we need for Clique and Interview"; the full-brief Clique record's remaining capability lines and Interview's adapter responsibilities). What it unblocks: Clique M5's three search scopes online and offline on one index, M6's push seam without connection leases hand-built in tables, M8's digest emitted rather than logged into a table, Interview's phone sign-in and model workers as a service that mints sessions instead of proxying every request, and the ranked feed of LLP 3000.004 on the `feeds` card. Taken off in the same change: "an embedded JS engine for effects, until measured" (the engine never calls out; a worker does), "word search" from the package's exclusions. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no relevance ranking, no identity provider in the engine.
- **2026-09-12 — expiry, assets and jobs move onto the doing-list** (author: "complete snapback4 to the point where the entire clique benchmark can be run"). What it unblocks: the struck half of the Clique brief (M4 media, M5 view-once and expiry with no client open, M8's scheduled digest). Taken off in the same change: the "one photo path" and "cron syntax" exclusions above narrow to transcoding and to cron beyond `every N [at HH:MM]`; the "time in rules" exclusion stands, answered by `expire at .col` rather than a rule.
- **2026-09-13 — native jobs on (author: "write up an LLP about this and then implement").** Unblocks the scoring half of a feed — floats, vectors, real loops, a typed schema — without putting any of it into Quarry, and gives the model half of LLP 3000.004 a seat inside the bounds: one chunk per commit, the job ceilings, fuel, no I/O. Off the doing-list in the same change: the "external ranker with a service seat" of LLP 3000.004 §6.5 is no longer the only shape for compute over the store; it remains the shape for the network. Ranked and vector search, effects execution and an embedded JS engine stay above.
- **2026-09-13 — `sql`, a fifth named observability verb, moves onto the doing-list** (author ruling on LLP 3000.007 §14.10). Read-only SQL over generated per-table views of the developer's own store, under `dev` or against a `--release` directory; never rule-filtered, never an application surface. What it unblocks: the ground truth two Clique essays said Snapback lacked beside Postgres ("there is no underlying Postgres to query"), and the inspection the 0.2.6 Astra lane did by copying its SQLite file. Taken off in the same change: SQL reads as an application query surface (LLP 3000.007 §9.1) leave the doing-list until the reporting gap is measured after the language relaxations; reporting is Quarry's, inspection is `sql`'s. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, one authoring language.

- **2026-09-14 — the simulator witness is published for app builders (LLP 3000.007 §20.10, S30).** Unblocks native offline proof artifacts; replaces the repository-only Metro witness on the doing-list. Real-browser and device witnesses still cover only what the deterministic simulator cannot reach.

- **2026-09-14 — development clock control (S27, calibration F5)** moves onto the doing-list to unblock expiry and scheduled-digest tests without real-minute waits or backdated seeds; waiting on real time leaves the development test path, while production clock controls stay off the doing-list.

- **2026-09-25 — ephemeral tables enact the standing realtime line of LLP 3000.009 §12 (LLP 3000.017).** Unblocks typing and viewing without durable outbox traffic. Nothing leaves the doing-list: this enacts an already admitted scope. Last-seen storage, multi-server presence, a separate presence stream and ephemeral authority over durable invariants remain excluded.

- **2026-09-30 — bounded client subscription inspection (author's PR round-0 task)**
  moves onto the doing-list: query-group queue/in-flight/withdrawal counts and
  bounded latency/cause samples explain mounted screens flashing empty. Taken off
  the doing-list: builders must reconstruct this pressure from ad-hoc console
  instrumentation. General observability beyond the named verbs remains excluded;
  there is no telemetry transport or server admin console (LLP 3000.023 §10).

Dormant row populations cover their own retained cells, bytes and assets; only
active rows contribute standalone column cells/samples. The archived-row example
is one row plus two cells. Audit keeps actual restoration counters and distinguishes
a truly empty definition from zero standalone cells covered by a dormant row.
The widening note says rows that later use those values. LLP 3000.000.000 §11
and the deploy card document the disjoint counts. Validation/review follows.

FIX28 review round 1: Muse found code sound but required final evidence; fresh
Codex found historical README recognition lost its old exact bytes and Rust
history lost accepted prerelease suffixes. Recognize old READMEs by ignoring
only the exact generated proof appendix (preserve edits), and distinguish
proof-only advice from offline changes. Align Rust/JS prerelease ordering,
including dotted/numeric suffixes without floating-point precision loss. Real
check regressions verify each stable history entry once after prereleases;
web/Expo regressions verify old README adaptation and edited-file preservation.
The first post-fix qualifier was interrupted through its owned tool session
before completing, so it supplies no successful qualification evidence.

FIX28 social proof closeout: fixing ?api= reveals the social template's Sign out
control is on Profile, while the generic proof remains on Following. A packed
consumer reproduction with only the URL fixed still times out; clicking Profile
through its rendered tab passes twice with occupied-port/CPU load. Emit that
step only for --template social; retain generic defaults/other templates. The
all-template regression checks its selection, and final packed matrices execute
it. The second qualifier was interrupted before completing and claims no pass.

FIX28 schema-less JSON control: its history was present both in a JSON field
and on stderr. Remove the redundant JSON history field; every upgrade/check
JSON path keeps history exclusively on stderr. An actual helper regression
first fails for duplicate output, then verifies one emission and silent repeat.
This is output custody only; no package/version/history interval change.
The prior qualification attempt was interrupted before completing; a fresh
exact packed-client run follows this final helper change.

FIX28 final qualification and reviews: semantic source `22e1fbf41a` keeps 0.4.28
and passes fresh `publish.mjs --prepare-client` plus Linux consumer qualification:
12 PROOF frontend attempts, the independent starter toolchain's 12 plus four
adapted attempts, and Ubuntu 24.04/Alpine installed consumers/all six toolchains.
Both fresh Muse and Codex reviews return READY on that revision. The affected
client run has 139 passes; dormant Rust 47; CLI binary 427/5 opt-in ignores,
check 101, check_notes 6; full init rerun 50, final init_dx3 7, guide 123; targeted
README/proof/history/lock controls pass. Root inventories and all 22 guide checks,
references and README 320/320 budget pass. Original reds and three interrupted
qualifiers remain distinct from final success. `reports/20261014-fix28/` retains
exact-byte/source bindings, complete proof matrices and hashed command logs.
Consumer evidence has publicationQualified false and suite not-run. No full
scripts/test.sh, native iOS/Mac publication qualification, upload or push.


### 2026-10-15 — Wave 12 LANG device budget

The locked Linux whole device at the LANG expiry/digest/language-runtime
revision measures 2,336,386 B raw, 712,744 B gzip and 538,230 B Brotli.
Strict wasm family and absence audits pass. The budget is 713,244 B:
measured gzip plus exactly 500 B, as requested for this workstream.
The 5,070 B gzip growth is measured against the qualified 0.4.28 device;
this whole-artifact measurement covers the shared runtime changes.
Final review, test and census evidence belongs in `reports/wave12-lang/`
and the worktree's `WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md`; qualification remains in progress.

-            || fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, right, rule, pins);
+    // The compiler lowers a left-associated `a and b and c` into successive
+    // Let/If pairs. Accept only that exact dataflow chain: every right operand
+    // assigns the current result on its true branch, while `or` assigns on the
+    // false branch and any extra statement or mismatched name is rejected.
+    if let Expr::Block { body, value } = filter {
+        if body.is_empty() || body.len() % 2 != 0 { return false; }
+        let mut previous: Option<&String> = None;
+        let mut matched = false;
+        let mut at = 0;
+        while at < body.len() {
+            let [crate::ir::Stmt::Let { name, value: left }, crate::ir::Stmt::If {
+                condition: Expr::Variable(condition), then_body, else_body }] = &body[at..at + 2] else { return false };
+            let [crate::ir::Stmt::Assign { name: assigned, value: right }] = then_body.as_slice() else { return false };
+            if !else_body.is_empty() || condition != name || assigned != name { return false; }
+            if let Some(previous) = previous {
+                if !matches!(left, Expr::Variable(input) if input == previous) { return false; }
+            } else {
+                matched |= fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, left, rule, pins);
+            }
+            matched |= fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, right, rule, pins);
+            previous = Some(name);
+            at += 2;
+        }
+        if !matches!((value.as_ref(), previous), (Expr::Variable(result), Some(previous)) if result == previous) { return false; }
+        return matched;
     }
     let (Expr::Exists(read), RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. }) = (filter, rule) else { return false };
     let Some(target) = schema.table(table) else { return false };

+/// Four binary equality factors require sixteen membership lanes and must not
+/// collapse into one undistributed complete descriptor after product overflow.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — alternative overflow retains the ordinary incomplete scan.
+pub fn page_membership_filter_overflow(seed: u64) -> Case {
+    let mut case = base(seed);

+    case.program.tables[0].effects = None;
+    case.program.tables[0].residency = Residency::Online;

+    let access = case.program.tables.iter_mut().find(|table| table.name == "access").unwrap();
+    access.rule = Rule::Allow; access.effects = None; access.residency = Residency::Public;
+    access.expire = None; access.not_before = None;
+    case.program.body = Body::Read { kind: Read::Page, take: 1, descending: seed % 2 == 0,
+        filter: false, owner: false, project: None };

+/// A shared equality supplies the physical prefix for every Cartesian lane;

+    case.program.tables[0].effects = None;
+    case.program.tables[0].residency = Residency::Online;
+    case.program.tables[0].columns.push(column("region", Type::Text));
+    case.program.tables[0].columns.push(column("rank", Type::Int));
+    let access = case.program.tables.iter_mut().find(|table| table.name == "access").unwrap();

+    case.program.body = Body::Read { kind: Read::Page, take: 1, descending: seed % 2 == 0,
+        filter: false, owner: false, project: None };
+    case.program.domain = Some(".region = 'north'".into());
+    case.program.residual = Some("(.score > 0 or .score > 10) and (.rank > 0 or .rank > 10)".into());

+    case.program.named_page_order = None; case.program.prelude.clear(); case.program.lets.clear(); case.steps.clear();

+        row["region"] = json!(if seed % 3 == 0 { "south" } else { "north" });
+        row["rank"] = json!((seed % 15) as i64);
+    }}
+    case

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/smoke.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/smoke.rs
index 7d6a20da18..f27427ef2c 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/smoke.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/smoke.rs
@@ -796,3 +796,33 @@ fn membership_page_with_two_equality_alternative_factors_is_complete_in_lean_and
         }
     }
 }
+
+#[test]
+fn page5_review_boundaries_agree_in_generated_native_and_lean_cases() {
+    let have_model = model_built();
+    for seed in 0..4 {
+        for (case, complete, model_boundary) in [
+            (snapback4_difftest::generate::page_membership_filter_overflow(seed), false, Some("page-membership-filter-product")),
+            (snapback4_difftest::generate::page_membership_shared_filter_product(seed), true, None),
+            (snapback4_difftest::generate::page5_implicit_id_vacancy(seed), true, None),
+        ] {
+            let source = case.program.source();
+            execute::compile(&source).unwrap_or_else(|error| panic!("seed {seed}: {error}\n{source}"));
+            let mut engine = execute::engine(&case, &source).unwrap();
+            let outcome = execute::execute(&mut engine, &case, "run", &case.args);
+            assert_eq!(outcome.value["complete"], complete, "seed {seed}: {outcome:?}\n{source}");
+            let mut report = Report::default(); campaign::check(&case, &mut report, |_| {});
+            assert!(!report.failed(), "seed {seed}: {report:?}\n{source}");
+            if have_model {
+                let checked = snapback4_difftest::lean::compare(&case, &source).unwrap();
+                if let Some(reason) = model_boundary {
+                    assert!(matches!(&checked.verdict, Verdict::Unsupported(actual) if actual == reason),
+                        "seed {seed}: {:?}\n{source}", checked.verdict);
+                    assert_eq!(checked.executed, 0, "seed {seed}: model boundary executed\n{source}");
+                } else {
+                    assert!(matches!(checked.verdict, Verdict::Pass), "seed {seed}: {:?}\n{source}", checked.verdict);
+                }
+            }
+        }
+    }
+}

 //! Physical page eligibility derived solely from Quarry surface declarations.
 use super::*;
 
+enum PagePins {
+    Plain(Vec<String>),
+    Product { system: Vec<String>, branches: Vec<Vec<String>> },
+    Invalid,
+}
+
+/// Reconstruct the native planner's bounded Cartesian product from the
+/// authored filter.  This stays surface-derived: the executable model cannot
+/// trust the binder's proof metadata when deciding whether comparison is safe.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — every modeled PAGE filter lane has its own exact physical prefix.
+fn page_pins(table: &Table, read: &ReadExpr) -> PagePins {
+    let Some(filter) = read.filter.as_deref() else { return PagePins::Plain(Vec::new()) };
+    let mut conjuncts = Vec::new();
+    flatten_and(filter, &mut conjuncts);
+    let system = model_pin_columns(table, &conjuncts, &read.order_keys);
+    let mut branches = vec![Vec::new()];
+    let mut distributed = false;
+    for conjunct in &conjuncts {
+        let mut alternatives = Vec::new();
+        flatten_or(conjunct, &mut alternatives);
+        distributed |= alternatives.len() > 1;
+        if branches.len().saturating_mul(alternatives.len()) > 8 { return PagePins::Invalid; }
+        branches = branches.into_iter().flat_map(|prefix| alternatives.iter().map(move |alternative| {
+            let mut lane = prefix.clone();
+            flatten_and(alternative, &mut lane);
+            lane
+        })).collect();
+    }
+    if !distributed || branches.len() < 2 { return PagePins::Plain(system); }
+    if branches.iter().any(|branch| !branch.iter().any(|atom| page_pin(table, atom))) {
+        return PagePins::Invalid;
+    }
+    PagePins::Product {
+        system,
+        branches: branches.into_iter().map(|branch| model_pin_columns(table, &branch, &read.order_keys)).collect(),
+    }
+}
+
+fn page_pin(table: &Table, expression: &Expr) -> bool {
+    let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = expression else { return false };
+    if op != "=" { return false; }
+    let (column, value) = if let Some(column) = column_of(left) { (column, right.as_ref()) }
+        else if let Some(column) = column_of(right) { (column, left.as_ref()) }
+        else { return false };
+    column != "id" && model_independent(value) && table.cols.iter().find(|field| field.name == column)
+        .is_some_and(|field| !field.nullable || matches!(value, Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "viewer"))
+}
