            scan.after = None;
            let mut conjuncts = Vec::new();
            if let Some(filter) = &read.filter { super::super::intent::split(*filter.clone(), &mut conjuncts); }
            let guard = super::super::intent::conjunction(conjuncts.into_iter().filter(super::super::intent::independent).collect());
            let guard = guard.map(|guard| self.expr(&guard, out).map(|(value, _)| value)).transpose()?;
            let mut projection_setup = setup::Body::default();
            let project_binding = if plain { self.temp("view_row") } else { "$project".into() };
            let (project, ty) = self.shape_object(&project_binding, Ty::Row(read.table.clone()), lane.project.as_ref().expect("view projection"), &lane.site, &mut projection_setup)?;
            if plain {
                scan.merge_site = None;
                scan.after = cursor.as_ref().map(|(value, _)| Box::new(value.clone()));
                let max = scan.take.maximum() as u32;
                let target = self.temp("view_rows");
                out.extend(setup);
                out.push(Stmt::Let { name: target.clone(), value: Expr::Array(Vec::new()) });
                projection_setup.push(Stmt::Push { target: target.clone(), value: project });
                out.push_for(project_binding, LoopDomain::Page(scan), projection_setup);
                self.pop_scope();
                return Ok((ir::var(&target), Ty::Collection(Box::new(ty), max)));
            }
            let fields = match ty { Ty::RowShape(fields) | Ty::RowObject(fields) | Ty::Object(fields) => fields, _ => unreachable!("shape object") };
            if let Some(prior) = &mut result {
                if prior.keys().ne(fields.keys()) {
                    return Err(type_error(&lane.site, format!("view {} branch fields differ from branch at {}", merge.table, first_site.as_ref().unwrap()), "project the same named fields in every view branch").guide("reads"));
                }
                for (name, ty) in fields {
                    let old = &prior[&name];
                    let current = projection_source(lane, &name);
                    let origins = field_sources.get_mut(&name).unwrap();
                    origins.push((ty.clone(), current));
                    old.join(&ty).map_err(|error| self.join_error_many(error.at(&name), origins))?;
                    let joined = view_join(old, &ty).ok_or_else(|| type_error(&lane.site,
                        format!("view {} field {name}: {} conflicts with {} at {}", merge.table, ty.name(), old.name(), first_site.as_ref().unwrap()),
                        format!("project compatible values for {name} in every branch")).guide("reads"))?;
                    prior.insert(name, joined);
                }
            } else {
                first_site = Some(lane.site.clone());
                field_sources = fields.keys().map(|name| (name.clone(), vec![(fields[name].clone(), projection_source(lane, name))])).collect();
                result = Some(fields);
            }
            let project = if projection_setup.is_empty() { project } else { Expr::Block { body: projection_setup.code, value: Box::new(project) } };
            self.pop_scope();
            self.push_value_lane(&mut lanes, lane, MergeLane { guard, distinct, branch: lane.branch.clone(), setup: setup.code, scan, source, binding, project: Some(project) }, position)?;
        }
        if result.is_none() && !lanes.is_empty() {
            let planned = Merge { variant: self.variant(), position: None, view: None, order_keys: Vec::new(), site, lanes };
            // @ref LLP 3000.036.001#3 — the binding check: the runtime's own analysis must agree that every
            // lane is decided (a grant lane by a grant covering the rule) and that the lanes share one
            // position. Otherwise a page with a grant branch reads as written, the grant branch alone.
            if planned.branches() && !snapback4_core::engine::rules::branch_merge_lazy(self.schema, &planned)
                && let Some(read) = merge.lanes.iter().find(|lane| matches!(lane.branch.as_deref(), Some(ir::GRANT_LANE | ir::SYSTEM_LANE))).and_then(|lane| lane.planned.as_deref()) {
                return self.read(read, out);
            }
            return Ok((Expr::Merge(planned), Ty::Page(merge.table.clone(), merge.take)));
        }
        let fields = match self.catalog.view_types.get(&merge.table) {
            Some(Ty::RowShape(fields)) => fields.clone(), _ => result.unwrap_or_default(),
        };
        let mut names = merge.view_order.clone();
        // Keep the projected order aligned with each branch's source scan,
        // including an explicitly ordered id before another field.
        if !names.iter().any(|name| name == "id") { names.push("id".into()); }
        let mut suffix = names.into_iter().map(|name| {
            let kind = fields.get(&name).and_then(cursor_kind).ok_or_else(|| type_error(&merge.site,
                format!("view order .{name} has no scalar column type"), "order by a field projecting a scalar source column in every branch"))?;
            Ok(snapback4_core::cursor::CursorColumn { name, kind })
        }).collect::<Fallible<Vec<_>>>()?;
        suffix.push(snapback4_core::cursor::CursorColumn { name: "$branch".into(), kind: snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Text { max: 1024 } });
        let position = ir::ViewPosition { take: merge.take, order: if merge.order == ast::Order::Asc { Order::Asc } else { Order::Desc }, after: cursor.map(|(value, _)| Box::new(value)), suffix };
        self.view_types.insert(merge.table.clone(), Ty::RowShape(fields.clone()));
        Ok((Expr::Merge(Merge { variant: self.variant(), position: Some(position), view: Some(merge.table.clone()), order_keys: merge.view_order.clone(), site, lanes }), Ty::Collection(Box::new(Ty::RowShape(fields)), merge.take)))
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.013#2.1 — nullable membership is an ordinary filtered lane,
    // below source authorization; no null value is used as an index prefix.
    fn push_value_lane(&mut self, lanes: &mut Vec<MergeLane>, ast: &ast::MergeLane, mut lane: MergeLane, position: prefix::Position) -> Fallible<()> {
        if let Some((_, read)) = &ast.nulls
            && !matches!(&lane.source, Some(ir::LaneSource::Range { .. })) {
            let Some(ir::LaneSource::List { value, max }) = lane.source.take() else {
                return Err(type_error(&ast.site, "nullable in needs a bounded list", "use a bounded list argument or expression after in"));
            };
            let values = self.temp("lane_values");
            let nulls = self.temp("null_lane");
            let item = self.temp("lane_value");
            // Evaluate the list once, inside lane discovery so a cut/cap keeps
            // its existing incompleteness latch. Record at most one null lane.
            lane.source = Some(ir::LaneSource::List { max, value: Expr::Block { body: vec![
                Stmt::Let { name: values.clone(), value },
                Stmt::Let { name: nulls.clone(), value: Expr::Array(Vec::new()) },
                Stmt::For { binding: item.clone(), domain: LoopDomain::ArgList { name: values.clone(), max }, body: vec![
                    Stmt::If { condition: Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::Eq, left: Box::new(ir::var(&item)), right: Box::new(ir::lit(Value::Null)) },
                        then_body: vec![Stmt::Push { target: nulls.clone(), value: ir::lit(Value::Bool(true)) }, Stmt::Break], else_body: Vec::new() },
                ] },
            ], value: Box::new(ir::var(&values)) } });
            let mut setup = setup::Body::default();
            let mut read = *read.clone();
            if let Some(range) = &mut read.range { range.cursor = None; }
            let (mut scan, _) = self.scan_at(&read, &mut setup, position)?;
            scan.after = lane.scan.after.clone();
            scan.opaque_cursor = lane.scan.opaque_cursor;
            scan.merge_site = lane.scan.merge_site.clone();
            scan.take = lane.scan.take.clone();
            let null_lane = MergeLane { guard: lane.guard.clone(), distinct: Some(ir::lit(Value::Bool(true))),
                branch: lane.branch.clone(), project: lane.project.clone(), setup: setup.code, scan,
                source: Some(ir::LaneSource::List { value: ir::var(&nulls), max: 1 }), binding: item };
            lanes.push(lane);
            lanes.push(null_lane);
        } else { lanes.push(lane); }
        Ok(())
    }
}

fn view_join(a: &Ty, b: &Ty) -> Option<Ty> {
    let optional = a.is_optional() || b.is_optional();
    let a = a.required();
    let b = b.required();

/bin/bash -lc "git show c6f616e7f1:snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/branch_merge.rs | sed -n '1,260p'" in /home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-lang
 succeeded in 0ms:
//! A query page planned as the k-way merge of its disjunctive branches
//! (LLP 3000.036.001): `tasks where .owner = viewer or .assignee = viewer`, or
//! the same list read through `read <- .owner = viewer or .assignee = viewer`.
//! Each lane is an index range of one table whose prefix decides the read
//! rule; lanes may read different indexes. Every lane resumes on its own
//! index from one shared position, the order columns then `id`, which
//! totally orders the union, so a row matching several branches has one
//! position and is emitted once.
use super::*;

/// One opened lane: its scan (without the shared cursor), evaluated prefix
/// and bounds, the rows read ahead, whether the range is exhausted, and the
/// grant verdict's deadline when a grant admitted it.
struct Lane<'a> {
    /// The compiled scan can contain arbitrarily large filter literals. It is
    /// shared by every discovered membership lane; only evaluated keys and
    /// bounds are retained per lane.
    scan: &'a Scan,
    prefix: Vec<Value>,
    bounds: Bounds,
    buffer: VecDeque<Value>,
    done: bool,
    grant: Option<super::super::expiry::Pending>,
    vacancy: bool,
    metered: bool,
    position_len: usize,
    /// The last position this lane examined (read or rejected by the
    /// filter), when it has examined anything past the cursor.
    through: Option<Vec<Value>>,
}

impl Runner<'_> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.001#3 — the lazy merge across indexes: one heap of lane heads, one shared position.
    pub(super) fn merge_branches(&mut self, merge: &Merge) -> Result<Vec<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        let first = &merge.lanes[0].scan;
        let site = merge.site.as_str();
        let metered_merge = merge.lanes.iter().any(MergeLane::membership);
        let table = self.schema.table(&first.table).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {}", first.table)))?;
        let index = table.index(&first.index).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing index {}.{}", first.table, first.index)))?;
        let take = self.take(&first.take)?;
        let order = first.order;
        let suffix = if metered_merge { word_heap::suffix(&mut self.meter, table, index, first.prefix.len(), true)? }
            else { Self::merge_suffix(table, index, first.prefix.len()) };
        if metered_merge { self.meter.heap(word_heap::allocation(crate::ir::BRANCH_DESCRIPTOR.len() + 64))?; }
        let descriptor = merge.branch_descriptor();
        // Every lane's evaluated pins, in lane order: the token's scope, so a
        // cursor never resumes a page whose arguments or viewer changed
        // (Muse round 3, finding 1), as a direct page's scope does.
        if metered_merge { self.meter.heap(word_heap::slots::<Vec<Value>>(merge.lanes.len()))?; }
        let mut prefixes = Vec::with_capacity(merge.lanes.len());
        for lane in &merge.lanes {
            let scan = match lane.source.as_ref().and_then(LaneSource::scan) { Some(source) if lane.membership() => source, _ => &lane.scan };
            let prefix = self.branch_prefix_values(scan, metered_merge);
            let prefix = if metered_merge {
                let Some(prefix) = self.membership_discovery_steps(prefix)? else { return Ok(Vec::new()); };
                prefix
            } else { prefix? };

        }
        if metered_merge { self.meter.heap(word_heap::slots::<Value>(1) + word_heap::allocation(64))?; }

                    if metered_merge { self.meter.heap(word_heap::cursor_decode(&token) + word_heap::text(&token))?; }

                    crate::cursor::check_variant(&position, merge.variant)?;

                        }

            let next = next.filter(|_| self.word_cut_at.is_none());
            if metered_merge { self.charge_branch_page(first, site, &descriptor, &page, next.as_ref())?; }
            self.record_page(site.to_owned(), PagePosition { variant: merge.variant, view_suffix: Some(suffix), table: first.table.clone(), index: descriptor, order,

    /// The page and its continuation candidate (`None` when it ends).
    fn merge_branch_lanes(&mut self, merge: &Merge, prefixes: Vec<Vec<Value>>, take: usize, after: Option<(Vec<Value>, bool)>) -> Result<(Vec<Value>, bool, Option<Vec<Value>>), RuntimeError> {

                let opened = self.open_branch_lane(lane, prefix, after.as_ref(), None, metered_merge)?;
                self.push_branch_lane(&mut lanes, opened, metered_merge, site)?; covered = true; continue;
            }
            if lane.membership() {
                let prior = self.env.get_mut(&lane.binding).map(|slot| std::mem::replace(slot, Value::Null));
                let prefixes = (|| -> Result<Vec<Vec<Value>>, RuntimeError> {
                    let mut prefixes = Vec::new();
                    for value in self.lane_values(lane)? {
                        if let Some(slot) = self.env.get_mut(&lane.binding) { *slot = value; }
                        else {
                            self.meter.heap(word_heap::text(&lane.binding) + word_heap::insertion::<String, Value>(self.env.len()))?;
                            self.env.insert(lane.binding.clone(), value);
                        }
                        self.meter.at(site);

                        // source-field copies are allocated. The full bound
                        // row is conservative for a prefix that projects it.

                let Some(prefixes) = self.membership_discovery_steps(prefixes)? else { return Ok((Vec::new(), false, None)); };
                if self.value_source_incomplete || self.read_capped() { return Ok((Vec::new(), false, None)); }
                for prefix in prefixes {
                    let opened = self.open_branch_lane(lane, prefix, after.as_ref(), Some(Default::default()), true)?;
                    self.push_branch_lane(&mut lanes, opened, true, site)?;
                }

            }

            let index = &definition.indexes[&lane.scan.index];

            if lane.grant() || lane.guarded() {

                // held authorized rows; it never decides a remote role probe.
                // scan_consumed still checks acquisition and meters the page.

                    .is_some_and(|sync| sync.is_union());

                        else { rules::covering_grant(self.schema, &lane.scan) };

                    // @ref LLP 3000.036.001#7 — a device decides a seat (`viewer = 'service:x'`), which reads no row;
                    // a probe guard (a role, a pinned parent) it does not decide (LLP 3000.036 §12.1): the read is unknown and the client asks the server.
                    if self.device && rules::rule_probes(&rule) > 0 {

        let batch = take + 1;
        let mut heap = BinaryHeap::new();
        for position in 0..lanes.len() {
            // A lane the meter never reached has examined nothing.
            if self.read_capped() { break; }
            self.fill_branch(&mut lanes[position], batch)?;
            let lane = &mut lanes[position];
            if let Some(row) = lane.buffer.pop_front() {
                self.meter.step(1)?;
                self.charge_branch_position(lane, &row)?;
                let key = self.branch_position(lane, &row);
                Self::push_branch_head(&mut self.meter, &mut heap, MergeHead { key, row, lane: position, order }, metered_merge)?;
            }
        }

        // continues from there; with a lane that examined nothing, it ends.

/// `.col = viewer` (either side) on a principal column of the row.
fn party(table: &TableInfo, value: &Expr) -> Option<String> {
    let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = value else { return None };
    if op != "=" { return None; }
    let column = match (intent::column(left), intent::column(right)) {
        (Some(column), None) if is_viewer(right) => column,
        (None, Some(column)) if is_viewer(left) => column,
        _ => return None,
    };
    (table.columns.get(column)?.kind == TypeKind::Principal).then(|| column.to_owned())
}

/// A rule disjunct every row column of which the page pins: none for a role
/// or a seat, the parent's column for a parent probe under its pin.
pub(super) fn columns_within(value: &Expr, pinned: &[String]) -> bool {
    let mut copy = value.clone();
    let mut within = true;
    super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |value| {
        if let Expr::Member { base, name, .. } = value && matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Focal { .. }) && !pinned.contains(name) { within = false; }
        else if let Expr::Focal { .. } = value { /* reached through a member, checked there */ }
    });
    within && !has_focal_without_member(value)
}

/// A bare `.` (the whole row) is not a pinned column.
fn has_focal_without_member(value: &Expr) -> bool {
    match value {
        Expr::Focal { .. } => true,
        Expr::Member { base, .. } if matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Focal { .. }) => false,
        Expr::Member { base, .. } | Expr::Unary { value: base, .. } => has_focal_without_member(base),
        Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => has_focal_without_member(left) || has_focal_without_member(right),
        Expr::Exists { read, .. } => read.keys.iter().any(|key| has_focal_without_member(&key.value)) || read.filter.as_deref().is_some_and(has_focal_without_member),
        Expr::Call { args, .. } | Expr::Array { items: args, .. } => args.iter().any(has_focal_without_member),
        _ => false,
    }
}

/// A predicate whose evaluation never refuses and never reads: comparisons,
/// `in` and boolean operators over the row's own columns, arguments, literals
/// and `viewer`. Arithmetic (even `now - 7d`, which overflows at the clock's
/// bound), assertions, calls and reads are left to the single-index plan, and
/// so is a member of anything but the row (`.meta.ok`, `m.ok`), which binds to
/// a read of the referenced row that a meter can cut (GPT round 4, finding 1).
// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#2 — totality: no read, so a lane is cut only between candidates
pub(super) fn total(value: &Expr) -> bool {
    match value {
        Expr::Literal { .. } | Expr::Name { .. } | Expr::Focal { .. } => true,
        Expr::Member { base, .. } => matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Focal { .. }),
        Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } => op == "not" && total(value),
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } => matches!(op.as_str(), "=" | "!=" | "<" | "<=" | ">" | ">=" | "and" | "or" | "in") && total(left) && total(right),
        Expr::Array { items, .. } => items.iter().all(total),
        _ => false,
    }
}

fn is_viewer(value: &Expr) -> bool { matches!(value, Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "viewer") }

fn viewer_pin(column: &str, site: &Site) -> Expr {
    Expr::Binary { op: "=".into(), site: site.clone(),
        left: Box::new(Expr::Member { base: Box::new(Expr::Focal { site: site.clone() }), name: column.into(), site: site.clone() }),
        right: Box::new(Expr::Name { name: "viewer".into(), site: site.clone() }) }
}

   472	    let pinned: Vec<String> = conjuncts.iter().filter_map(|conjunct| intent::equality(conjunct, table).map(|(column, _)| column)).collect();
   473	    let mut parties = Vec::new();
   474	    for disjunct in &disjunct_list {
   475	        if let Some(column) = party(table, disjunct) { if !parties.contains(&column) { parties.push(column); } }
   476	        else if columns_within(disjunct, &pinned) { /* a guard: the grant lane reads it */ }
   477	        else { return None; }
   478	    }
   479	    // `exists` takes case 1 only: an `or` of party reads misses rows for the
   480	    // system, which every rule admits, and a guard lane has no existence form.
   481	    if parties.is_empty() || !grants { return None; }
   482	    let base = alternatives.unwrap_or_else(|| vec![conjuncts.clone()]);
   483	    // The grant lane, the read as written, is read for a caller its guards
   484	    // admit and for the system (which every rule admits); a rule of parties
   485	    // alone has no guard, so the lane is the system's only.
   486	    let mut out = vec![(conjuncts, true)];
   487	    for branch in &base {
   488	        for column in &parties {
   489	            let mut lane = vec![viewer_pin(column, &read.site)];
   490	            lane.extend(branch.iter().cloned());
   491	            out.push((lane, false));
   492	        }
   493	    }
   494	    (out.len() <= MAX_BRANCHES).then_some(out)
   495	}
   496	
   497	/// The bounded positive alternatives of the read's top-level conjunction.
   498	/// Each `or` contributes a Cartesian factor, so
   499	/// `(a = x or b = x) and (state = 'open' or state = 'held')` becomes four
   500	/// independently pinned lanes.  Keep the published eight-lane ceiling while
   501	/// allowing an exact query predicate to narrow a wider read-rule union.
   502	// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — exact positive query scopes may combine party and status alternatives.
   503	fn where_branches(table: &TableInfo, conjuncts: &[Expr]) -> Option<Vec<Vec<Expr>>> {
   504	    let mut branches = vec![Vec::new()];
   505	    let mut distributed = false;
   485	        }
   486	        false
   487	    }
   488	}
   489	
   490	/// Every fact the grant reads is one the caller may read itself.
   491	fn self_disclosing(schema: &Schema, grant: &RuleExpr) -> bool {
   492	    disclosing(schema, grant, false)
   493	}
   494	
   495	/// A disjunct whose row columns a page pins (`exists cats[.cat, owner =
   496	/// viewer]` under `.cat = cat`): whenever it holds, the caller can read every
   497	/// row it probes, so a program's own `exists cats[cat, owner = viewer]` (which
   498	/// sees only readable rows) answers exactly as the rule does. Pinned columns
   499	/// are arbitrary values here: they pin nothing on the probed row's rule.
   500	// @ref LLP 3000.036#16.1 — a printed branch on a parent probe must not narrow the probe's holders.
   501	pub fn pinned_probe_readable(schema: &Schema, disjunct: &RuleExpr) -> bool {
   502	    disclosing(schema, disjunct, true)
   503	}
   504	
   505	fn disclosing(schema: &Schema, grant: &RuleExpr, fields: bool) -> bool {
   506	    match grant {
   507	        RuleExpr::Allow | RuleExpr::Public { .. } => true,
   508	        RuleExpr::Eq(a, b) | RuleExpr::Ne(a, b) => [a, b].iter().all(|term| matches!(term, RuleTerm::Viewer | RuleTerm::Literal(_)) || fields && matches!(term, RuleTerm::Field(_))),
   509	        RuleExpr::And(items) => items.iter().all(|item| disclosing(schema, item, fields)),
   510	        // @ref LLP 3000.036.001#17 — negative evidence is admissible only on
   511	        // a total, readable, lifetime-free point; hidden probes stay partial.
   512	        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => if let RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, .. } = inner.as_ref() {
   513	            schema.maintain_for(table).is_none() && schema.table(table).is_some_and(|table| table.index(index).is_some_and(|index|
   514	                index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.components.iter().all(|part|
   515	                    part.key_type(table).is_some_and(|kind| !kind.is_optional()))))
   516	                && disclosing(schema, inner, fields)
   517	        } else { false },
   518	        RuleExpr::Exists { table: target, index, key, equalities, .. } => {
   519	            let Some(target_table) = schema.table(target) else { return false };
   520	            let Some(target_index) = target_table.index(index) else { return false };
   521	            if target_table.through.is_some() || target_table.read_lifetime_sensitive() { return false; }
   522	            let value = |term: &RuleTerm| match term {
   523	                RuleTerm::Viewer => Some(Expr::Input(Input::Viewer)),
   524	                RuleTerm::Literal(value) => Some(Expr::Literal(value.clone())),
   525	                _ => None,
   526	            };
   527	            let mut pins: BTreeMap<String, Expr> = BTreeMap::new();
   528	            for (component, term) in target_index.components.iter().zip(key) {
   529	                if fields && matches!(term, RuleTerm::Field(_)) && component.column().is_some() { continue; }
   530	                let (Some(column), Some(expr)) = (component.column(), value(term)) else { return false };
   531	                pins.insert(column.clone(), expr);
   532	            }
   533	            for (column, term) in equalities {
   534	                if fields && matches!(term, RuleTerm::Field(_)) { continue; }
   535	                let Some(expr) = value(term) else { return false };
   536	                pins.insert(column.clone(), expr);
   537	            }
   538	            let pinned = |name: &str| pins.get(name);
   539	            // The witness row's own read rule, decided by what the probe pins,
   540	            // or carrying this very probe as a disjunct (`read <- exists staff[viewer]`).
   541	            rule_true(&target_table.rules.read, target, target_table, &pinned)
   542	                || target_table.rules.read.disjuncts().iter().any(|disjunct| implied_by(disjunct, grant))
   543	        }
   544	        _ => false,
   545	    }
   546	}
   547	
   548	/// `weaker` holds whenever `stronger` does: the same probe with fewer extra equalities.
   549	fn implied_by(weaker: &RuleExpr, stronger: &RuleExpr) -> bool {
   550	    match (weaker, stronger) {
   551	        (RuleExpr::Exists { table: a, index: ai, key: ak, equalities: ae, .. }, RuleExpr::Exists { table: b, index: bi, key: bk, equalities: be, .. }) =>
   552	            a == b && ai == bi && ak == bk && ae.iter().all(|pair| be.contains(pair)),
   553	        _ => weaker == stronger,
   554	    }
   555	}
the whole page; not held, it contributes nothing and the owner branch is the
page. A staff page is complete for staff (as before) and an owner's page is
complete for everyone else (new). The guide's owner-or-staff and two-party
recipes are now single queries.

## 1. What the builders met

LLP 3000.036 records the shape in waves 2–6: `#12` (12 lanes), `#3b`
(19 lanes) and the §14.2 and §16.1 notes (16 lanes). The lanes either pasted
the per-party rewrite (two queries, two cursors, a client-side merge and
de-duplication, two loading states) or kept the one-query form and shipped a
list that always said "there may be more". The engine already merges ordered
lanes (LLP 3000 §7, 2026-09-13) and already has the position that makes a
merge resumable (LLP 3000.007.000 §1); this RFC lets one merge read several
indexes of one table.

## 2. The rule

Definitions, over the checked schema:

- An **equality pin** in a conjunction is a conjunct `.col = e` (either
  side) or a bare boolean column (`.flag`, `not .flag`), where `e` reads no
  row column, `.col` is a plain column of the table and not `id`. On a
  nullable column it counts only when `e` is `viewer`: a branch never seeks a
  null the original filter would reject.
- A **party disjunct** of a read rule is `.col = viewer` (either side) on a
  principal column of the row.
- A **guard disjunct** reads no column of the row (§12.1: a role probe, a
  seat such as `viewer = 'service:audit'`), or reads only columns the page's
  own pins fix (§16.3: `exists boards[.board, owner = viewer]` under `.board =
  b`). It is **usable** when `rules::page_guard_decision` takes it: no `not`,
  and every row it probes readable by whoever its key names.
- A prefix **decides** the read rule when every column the rule reads is
  pinned (with the self-witness proof of LLP 3000.007 §16), or the rule is
  true by the pins' form: `allow`, a `.col = viewer` disjunct pinned to
  `viewer`, a `.col = 'x'` disjunct pinned to `'x'`, and `and`/`or` of
  those. This is the core's `rules::rule_true`/`scan_visibility`, the
  binder's `intent_branches::decided` and the model's `decidedPins`.
- A predicate is **total** when it is comparisons (`=`, `!=`, `<`, `<=`,
  `>`, `>=`, `in`) and boolean operators over the row's own columns,
  arguments, literals and `viewer`: its evaluation never refuses and never
  reads. A member of anything but the row (`.meta.ok`, `m.ok`) is not: it
  binds to a read of the referenced row. CUT (2026-10-12) fixes interrupted
  single-index predicates by retrying their row inclusively (LLP 3000.007
  §20.24.1), but does not make such a predicate total: missing/hidden targets
  can still refuse or be unavailable, and distribution changes which reads
  short-circuit. Keep this exclusion and the merge's decided lane frontier.
  Arithmetic is not either,
  not even a time window (`now - 7d` overflows when the clock is at the
  integer bound), so such a page keeps one index. The Lean model has no `in`;
  the emitter names it unsupported.

A **query page** (`t where … first|last N by … [after c]` in a query program;
never in a mutation, job, shared helper or shape, which keep ordinary scans)
is **branch-decided** when all of these hold:

- it names no explicit index or key, does not search (`matching`), has no
  computed order, and reads an ordinary table (not a maintained view, row
  view, ephemeral or timeline table, a `public` declaration or a `read
  through` child), whose read rule is one `read` declaration;
- its own pins do **not** decide the rule (a decided page keeps its plan,
  index and cursor);
- its predicate is total;
- and either
  1. **branches in `where`:** exactly one top-level conjunct is an `or` of
     2–8 alternatives, each holding an equality pin, and each alternative
     with the other conjuncts decides the rule; or
  2. **branches in the read rule:** the rule is an `or` of at least two
     disjuncts, each a party or a usable guard, with at least one party.
     The branches are `base_j and .party_k = viewer` for every base branch
     (case 1's alternatives when `where` has such an `or`, else the whole
     `where`) and party, plus one **grant branch**, the whole `where`, read
     for a caller the rule's guards admit and for the system, which every
     rule admits (a rule of parties alone has no guard: the lane is the
     system's only);
- and every branch plans to an index of at most 8 components whose position
  after its pins is the page's order then `id` (a branch never pins an order
  column: a merge lane keeps such an equality as a seek), with at most 8
  branches.

Otherwise the page keeps its single-index plan and, when undecided,
`N_NEVER_COMPLETE` with its per-party rewrite.

A single party (`read <- .owner = viewer`) is not expanded: its complete form
is the pin the author writes, and expanding it would replan every unpinned
owner page (§12 Q1). 8 is both the index width limit and the point below
which the lazy merge reads a page per lane (`word_merge.rs`); every branch
may be a synthesized index.

### 2.1 Why the merged rows are exactly the readable matches

Write `W` for the predicate and `R` for the read rule; the page returns, in
order, the rows `r` with `W(r) ∧ R(r)`.

- Case 1: `W = rest ∧ (a_1 ∨ … ∨ a_k)`, so `W = ∨_j (a_j ∧ rest)`. Branch
  `j` reads the range its pins select and filters by the rest of `a_j ∧
  rest`; its prefix decides `R`, so every row of the branch is readable
  (verdict true) or none is (verdict false: the branch is empty). The union
  is `∨_j (a_j ∧ rest ∧ R) = W ∧ R`.
- Case 2: `R = ∨_k (.p_k = viewer) ∨ G` with `G` row-independent or reading
  only the page's pinned columns, so for every row the page selects and this
  caller `R(r) = ∨_k (r.p_k = viewer) ∨ g` with `g` one boolean (the guard
  with the pinned values), and
  `W ∧ R = ∨_{j,k} (b_j ∧ .p_k = viewer) ∨ (g ∧ W)`. Each party branch is
  decided true by its pin; the grant branch is all of `W` when `g` holds and
  nothing otherwise, and then it is a superset of every other branch, so the
  plan reads it alone. The Lean model proves the party half
  (`parties_cover_view`): every visible row carries the viewer in a party
  column.

The argument distributes `W` over its alternatives, so it needs `W` total:
with `or (.assignee = viewer and 1 / n = 1)`, a row in both parties skips the
division in the original and would evaluate it in its own branch (GPT
review, finding 1). A branch evaluates the predicate only on rows the
original also reads, so a total predicate gives the same rows.

A row in several branches has the same position in each (§5), so the merge
emits it once. No branch reads a row the caller may not read, so no such row
reaches the merge, the filter, the lookahead or the cursor.

## 3. The plan

**Planning** (`bind/intent_branches.rs`, called from `intent::plan` where the
other lane expansions run). Each branch is an ordinary planned read
(`Requests::plan_lane`): its pins form the prefix (a party pin first, so the
deciding pin is never left residual), the page's order then `id` the suffix;
the planner reuses a declared index with exactly those columns or synthesizes
one. The expansion is dropped, and the read planned as written, when a branch
does not decide the rule (by the binder's check), the branches' order columns
differ, an index would exceed 8 components, or a branch exceeds the portable 2,048-byte key or 512-byte label bound,
or planning a branch raised a diagnostic: a program that compiled before still compiles. The result is the
existing `MergeExpr` with planned lanes, each carrying the branch identity
`$or` (or `$or:grant`) in the field views use for theirs; the planned-merge
binding passes it through to the IR `MergeLane.branch`. No IR type gains a
field, so every existing program's IR and pinned digest is unchanged;
`Merge::branches()` recognises the plan.

**Binding check** (`program/views.rs`). With the core schema in hand, the
binder re-checks the plan with the runtime's own functions: every grant
lane's `rules::covering_grant` (every disjunct that is not a party is a
usable guard of `page_guard_decision` for the lane's pins, §12.1, §16.3) and `rules::branch_merge_lazy` (same table and order, scalar
pins, every non-grant lane `scan_visibility(..).uniform`, a total position,
identical position columns). `covering_grant` compares each non-party
disjunct after the same prefix simplification that produced the page guard;
`allow`, `deny`, `not` and required boolean pins therefore have one identity
through the coverage proof, while a nullable boolean's `null` is never
normalized to `false`. If it fails and the plan has a grant lane, the
read binds as its grant lane alone, which is the read as written: the grant
lane is planned exactly as the single-index page is (not as a merge lane), so
the fallback is 0.4.23's plan, page guard included; when that plan's order
differs from the party lanes' (an equality on the first order column becomes
a pin there), the page is not merged at all (a hidden
roster, `read <- deny` on `staff`, keeps today's plan and note); without a
grant lane the merge stays, sound and, for a lane that is not decided,
incomplete.

**Execution** (`engine/branch_merge.rs`). A branch merge that passes
`branch_merge_lazy` is a lazy merge whose lanes may read different indexes of
one table. Each lane's position is its index components after its prefix,
plus `id` when they do not end in it (LLP 3000.007.000 §1); the binding check
makes the positions comparable, and because they end in the row id, equal
positions are the same row. Grant lanes open first. A grant lane's guard is
decided once with the lane's pinned values (`grant_verdict`, charged as §12.1) and, when it holds, the lane
reads as a decided page (`scan_consumed`'s grant arm, through the runner's
`merge_grant`) and the other lanes are not opened; when it does not hold, the
lane is not read and does not mark the page non-uniform; for the system it
reads every row. Each lane opens with one read of `take + 1` rows; the heap
pops in position order, de-duplicates by id and refills a lane strictly after
its last popped position. A branch merge that is not lazy-eligible takes the
eager path with the same branch descriptor; it is sound and, with a lane
that is not decided, incomplete with no `next`.

**Page guards (LLP 3000.036 §16.3).** Without the merge, 0.4.23 decides a
single-index page once for a caller a pinned guard admits, and walks it per
row (incomplete) for everyone else. With it, a page whose rule is parties and
such guards merges the guard branch with the party branches, so the guard's
holder reads the guard branch alone and everyone else their party branches,
all complete. A page the merge does not cover keeps the single-index guard.

  1361

  1365	within eight, and every original predicate stays in its lane. A product that

  1374	
  1375	**Fixed grants beside one varying grant.** One dynamic membership witness may

  1383	an already-static point such as `exists roles[viewer]` needs no substitution

  1385	guards; its exact short-circuit conjunction chain remains necessary in full.

  1391

  1399	
  1400	**Exact vacancy lanes.** The server may invert a top-level

## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

# LLP 2000.000: Snapback 3 — keep the core, flip two substrates, start at hub scale

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Draft
**Role:** Second opinion on LLP 2000; since 2026-09-12 also the design brief Snapback 4 builds (LLP 3000)
**Systems:** Snapback 3 product architecture, bounded computation, client data model, client API vocabulary, language and tooling, effects runtime, verification (simulation), performance budgets, process
**Author:** Claude Fable 5.1, at Charlie Cheever's request
**Date:** 2026-09-12
**Revised:** 2026-09-12 (adopted as the Snapback 4 design brief by LLP 3000 decision 3); 2026-09-12 (initial)
**Related:** [LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md) (parent), [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the Snapback 4 root that builds this brief), LLP 0000, [LLP 0329](./0329-agent-native-rebuild-diagnosis.research.md), [LLP 0329.014.004](./0329.014.004-bounded-data-programs-v1-design.rfc.md), [LLP 0329.016](./0329.016-agentic-dev-loop-from-scratch.rfc.md), [LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md), [LLP 1001](./1001-query-engine.spec.md), [LLP 1002](./1002-dev-loop.spec.md), [LLP 1012](./1012-local-first-v1.spec.md), [LLP 1018.003](./1018.003-truthful-and-composed-client.decision.md), [LLP 1018.008](./1018.008-elegance-after-the-corrections.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.007](./1018.008.007-affordable-offline-acquisition.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.008](./1018.008.008-locality-of-application-changes.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.009](./1018.008.009-residual-ergonomic-gaps.research.md), [LLP 1019](./1019-derived-delivery-custody.rfc.md), [LLP 1020](./1020-day-two-evolution-and-legible-ceilings.rfc.md), [LLP 1023](./1023-clique-builder-jump.rfc.md), [LLP 1024](./1024-single-lane-delivered-search.rfc.md), [LLP 1025](./1025-digest-final-emission-at-hub-scale.research.md), `rules/RULES.md`, `rules/NOT-DOING.md`, `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-05-clique-full-3x3/holistic-scores.md`

## 1. Summary

LLP 2000 is right that the whole application is the unit of design, right
that ownership comes before abstraction, and right to ask for deterministic
simulation and an early real deployment. Its weakness is that it treats the
two structural choices that produced Snapback 2's costs as open research
questions, and then proposes a first experiment at exactly the scale where
those costs are invisible. This document takes a position on both, names
what to keep from Snapback 2 verbatim, and rewrites the first slice so it
can fail for the right reasons.

The position, in one paragraph. Snapback 2's *core* is excellent and should
be copied: the rule language and its uniformity analysis, the structural-fact
family, one data language, typed states with no silence, refusals that paste
a rewrite, personas, the successor classifier, the derived per-recipient
log, the loop verbs and their sub-second budgets. Two *substrates* under
that core are where the record shows the hours went, and both should be
flipped rather than re-investigated: (1) the static certificate should keep
certifying **authorization and shape** and stop pricing **bytes and steps**,
which move to a deterministic runtime meter with honest `capped`/abort
outcomes; and (2) the client should hold **a replica of the viewer's declared
partition** — the local-first shape every shipped sync engine and Snapback
2's own `deliver` log converge on — instead of a per-query result cache
proven by receipts. Those two flips dissolve most of the machinery that
leaked into the application (receipts, coverage, halos, validators, grants,
ID slices, drains, `withHistory`, follower inboxes, per-table retention
words) and turn the 28-second acquisition into a bulk stream whose floor is
the device's write speed. The first slice should be built on the **hub
fixture** (1,000 memberships, 500 follows, 100,000 messages), with the
day-two change designed before the day-one screen, a native client in the
first slice, and a deterministic simulator as the primary test harness.

Three claims this document makes that LLP 2000 does not:

1. **The costs have two named causes, and both are decisions, not
   mysteries.** Byte-priced static certification and the receipt-proven
   result cache account for every ergonomic residual in LLP 1018.008.009
   except documentation. §2 shows the mapping.
2. **The first experiment must include the hub.** Snapback 2 was "elegant on
   day one and hostile on day two" (LLP 1020) and broke at 1,000 memberships
   (LLPs 1023, 1024, 1025). A two-principal slice will pass and teach nothing.
3. **The process is part of the product.** Seventy-one LLPs, over two
   thousand commits, and seven ceiling raises in fourteen days produced a
   sound system nobody can hold in their head. Snapback 3 needs one owner on
   the core, a simulator instead of a test matrix, and budgets anchored to
   competitors so that raising one means admitting a loss.

## 2. What the record says, read as causes

The evidence LLP 2000 §2 cites is right; its reading is too gentle. Every
row below is a Snapback 2 cost recorded in the corpus, with the substrate
that produced it. Numbers are the corpus's own and retain its scope limits.

| Recorded cost | Where | Cause |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Adding `editedAt: time ?` (44 bytes × 244 retained copies = 10,736) pushed `messages.searchAll` to 4,199,688 closure bytes against 4,194,304; the builder shrank a page from 20 to 19 | LLP 1018.008.008; 0.0.21 day-two diary | **Static byte pricing.** Closure bytes are a bound on what a receipt could ship; the product paid for the bound, not the work |
| The 1,000-membership digest fold certifies at 9,922,698 heap and 8,691,704 closure bytes and is refused; the recipient-keyed form is admitted at 2,733,253 / 1,848,418 | LLP 1025 §3 | **Static byte pricing** (heap = steps × 32 + closure scratch). A runtime meter would have run the fold and reported actual bytes |
| A message page is 50, not 200, because a row "prices at 16,016 bytes" and 200 exceed the 1 MiB output ceiling | LLP 1012 §3 | **Static byte pricing** of the worst-case row width |
| A per-recipient search projection is refused at 20,863,641 reserved steps against 1,000,000 | LLP 1024 §1 | **Static step pricing** of a derivation the engine would perform incrementally |
| Full acquisition of 10,008 messages: 67.4 s → 53.6 s → 32.6 s → 28.4 s against a 2–3 s objective; 201 requests, 200 acknowledgement mutations, 612 IndexedDB transactions; PowerSync reports 10,000 rows in 0.36 s native and 2.5 s on web | LLP 1018.008.007; LLP 1018.008.009 §4 | **Result cache, not replica.** Every 50-row page is a certified query, a receipt, a durable acknowledgement mutation, and a store swap |
| Adding a count to a thread makes the composite `partial` offline whenever count authority is missing, so the template declares an independent `history` query and `withHistory` bindings; 27 files / 1,718 lines → 28 / 1,751 after the fix | LLP 1018.008 F2; LLP 1018.008.009 §2 | **Result cache.** The count is not a fact the receipt covers, so the client cannot compose locally |
| `shared/thread.ts` describes table identity, row selection, order, create membership, draft fields and mutation targets a second time | LLP 1018.008 F2 | **Result cache.** Items must be reconstructed from answers because there is no local table to read |
| Sending stalled with 85 IDs remaining against a certified 352-ID slice on an 8,192-ID grant; recovery needed a "Sync now" action | LLP 1018.008 F1 | **Result cache + engine-minted IDs.** Prediction needs server ids before commit, so ids are granted in slices |
| A follower browser tab writes through a durable inbox row; the SDK carries `forwarding`, `writer-unresponsive`, `inbox-full`, `follower` states | LLP 1018.004 | **Result cache.** One writer per partition image; other tabs cannot mutate a receipt-proven image |
| Global search over 1,000 memberships costs ~1,010 HTTP requests through a client N-way merge | LLP 1024 §2 | **Result cache** (no local index over the partition) + **static pricing** (the server-side projection is refused) |
| The complete local runtime grew 7.8 KB → 89,843 gzip bytes; the ceiling was raised 34,000 → 50,000 → 66,000 → 82,000 → 82,500 → 84,000 → 85,000 → 90,000 | LLP 1000 §"Revised"; `rules/NOT-DOING.md` §Moves; LLP 1018.008 §10 | **Result cache.** The inputs list of `metrics/twin.jsonl` is coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, posting trees, fact integrity, swap journals — receipt machinery, not the interpreter |
| `Snapshot` carries `data, next, partial, receipt{state, source, seq, next, reason, rule, sites}, freshness{live, staleSince, connection}, local{predicted, retained, caughtUp, watermark}, refusal, status, reason` | LLP 1018.003 §1 | Both. Every field is a true fact about the mechanism; almost none is a fact about the application |
| `Schema::rule_hash` binds every queued write to the complete schema; an optional column on the subject table invalidates unrelated evidence | LLP 1018.002; LLP 1018.008.008 | **Result cache.** Evidence identity exists because held answers must be re-authorized; a replica re-runs the mutation at the server and needs no evidence identity |

Against that, what scored: on the 2026-09-05 three-scorer Clique run
Snapback 2 beat Convex on authorization (82 vs 79) and offline (81 vs 44)
and lost on queries and live (60 vs 85), client integration (59 vs 79),
setup and docs (55 vs 79), diagnostics and loop (66 vs 77), operations (53
vs 76) and scale (62 vs 80); overall 66 vs 81. The later single-lane 0.0.21
and 0.0.25 scores of 82 and 85 are real improvement and not a three-scorer
comparison. The wins are the core; the losses are composition, discovery,
scale and operations — the substrates and what sits on them.

LLP 2000 §2 summarises this as "costs arise where one layer must reconstruct
# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.

    fn merge_with_words(&mut self, merge: &Merge, word: Option<&WordRead>) -> Result<Vec<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        if merge.view.is_some() { return self.merge_view(merge); }
        if merge.branches() && rules::branch_merge_lazy(self.schema, merge) { return self.merge_branches(merge); }

Found one material completeness regression in `c6f616e7f1`.

**[P1] A readable static disjunct can conceal an uncovered hidden static disjunct.** The new condition at [intent_compound.rs:67](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-lang/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/intent_compound.rs:67) removes every singleton static probe from retained validation. However, [rules.rs:610](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-lang/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/rules.rs:610) accepts each resulting guarded lane when `page_guard_decision(...).is_some()`. That proves *some* disjunct is usable; it does not prove the omitted disjunct is covered.

Material counterexample: extend the new static+membership fixture with `secretRoles`, uniquely keyed by `principal`, with `read <- deny`, and use:

```quarry
read <- exists secretRoles[viewer] or exists roles[viewer, role = 'advisor'] or exists access[.id, viewer]
```

Seed an item and a `secretRoles` row for Alice, leaving `roles` and `access` empty. Source tracing shows:

- Both static leaves become guarded lanes without residual probes.
- `page_guard_decision` excludes `secretRoles` but returns the readable `roles` guard, so both lanes pass validation.
- At runtime both lanes evaluate that same false `roles` guard and remain closed. Membership discovery contributes nothing.
- The merge returns an empty complete page, although the hidden rule probe authorizes every item.

Before this delta, `total_readable(secretRoles…)` rejected the candidate. Require coverage of each omitted static alternative before accepting the merge.

The remaining review points:

- `leaf.len() == 1` correctly distinguishes singleton DNF alternatives from conjunction siblings. Conjunction siblings still follow retained-filter validation, including the prior left-associated chain handling.
- Core guarded-lane validation still runs, but its existential check is insufficient for completeness here.
- The three requested positive reductions are present. The new negative at [page4.rs:315](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-lang/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/tests/page4.rs:315) hides the **standalone** `roles` disjunct; its comment incorrectly describes a conjunction sibling. It misses the mixed readable+hidden static counterexample above.
- §18’s statement that an admitting standalone grant opens its range is therefore inconsistent with this implementation. I found no separate change to DNF bounds or conjunction retention.

This was a read-only source review of the committed versions; I ran no builds or tests.

VERDICT: NOT READY
